--- Strategy Report Received ---
# Scientific Strategy Report — 2D Rayleigh–Taylor Instability (Compressible Euler + Gravity)

---

## 1. Problem Statement

Solve the inviscid compressible Euler equations with a gravitational source term for the single-mode Rayleigh–Taylor instability. A heavy fluid ($\rho = 2.0$) rests below a light fluid ($\rho = 1.0$) across a smoothed interface at $y = 0.5$; gravity acts in $+y$, i.e., the acceleration is directed **from the heavy into the light fluid** — the RT-unstable configuration (Atwood number $A = 1/3$). A single-mode perturbation (one wavelength per domain width) seeds the instability. Objective: capture the full evolution — exponential linear growth → nonlinear spike/bubble formation → Kelvin–Helmholtz roll-up → late-time mixing — on $t \in [0, 3.0]$.

---

## 2. Mathematical Specification (Code-Ready Extraction)

* **Governing Equations:** 2D Compressible Euler with gravity source:
$$\partial_t \begin{pmatrix}\rho\\ \rho u\\ \rho v\\ E\end{pmatrix} + \partial_x \begin{pmatrix}\rho u\\ \rho u^2 + p\\ \rho u v\\ (E+p)u\end{pmatrix} + \partial_y \begin{pmatrix}\rho v\\ \rho u v\\ \rho v^2 + p\\ (E+p)v\end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix}0\\ 0\\ \rho g\\ \rho g\, v\end{pmatrix}$$
with equation of state:
$$p = (\gamma - 1)\left(E - \tfrac{1}{2}\rho\left(u^2 + v^2\right)\right)$$

* **Domain:** $(x,y) \in [0, 0.25] \times [0, 1.0]$, $\quad t \in [0, 3.0]$

* **Boundary Conditions:**
  * $x$-direction: Periodic.
  * $y$-direction: Reflective / free-slip walls at $y=0$ and $y=1$ (no-penetration: $v = 0$; mirror tangential velocity $u$).

* **Initial Conditions** (exact, for every field):
$$\rho(x,y,0) = 2.0 + (1.0 - 2.0)\cdot \tfrac{1}{2}\left[1 + \tanh\left(50\,(y - 0.5)\right)\right]$$
$$p(x,y,0) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1, & y < 0.5\\[2pt] y + 1.5, & y \geq 0.5\end{cases}$$
$$u(x,y,0) = 0$$
$$v(x,y,0) = -0.025\; c(x,y)\,\cos(8\pi x)\,\sin^6(\pi y), \qquad c(x,y) = \sqrt{\frac{\gamma\, p(x,y,0)}{\rho(x,y,0)}}$$
$$E(x,y,0) = \frac{p(x,y,0)}{\gamma - 1} + \frac{1}{2}\,\rho(x,y,0)\,\Big[u(x,y,0)^2 + v(x,y,0)^2\Big]$$

* **Constants & Parameters:** $\gamma = 1.4$; $g = 1.0$ (source adds momentum in $+y$); $\rho_{heavy} = 2.0$, $\rho_{light} = 1.0$; interface $y_i = 0.5$; smoothing steepness $= 50$; perturbation amplitude $= 0.025$; mode $k_x = 8\pi$ (wavelength $= 0.25$, exactly one period per domain); $\mu = 0$, $k_{\text{thermal}} = 0$.

* **Expected Physics:**
  * **Early ($t \lesssim 1$):** Exponential growth of the single mode (incompressible estimate $\sigma \approx \sqrt{A k g} \approx 2.9$); a small localized adjustment transient at the interface due to the tanh-smoothed $\rho$ vs. piecewise-linear $p$ mismatch — subsumed into the instability growth.
  * **Intermediate ($t \approx 1$–$2$):** Nonlinear saturation; heavy-fluid **spikes rise ($+y$)** near $x = 0.125$, light-fluid **bubbles sink ($-y$)** near $x = 0,\, 0.25$; shear layers on spike flanks seed Kelvin–Helmholtz roll-up.
  * **Late ($t \approx 2$–$3$):** Mushroom structures, vortex-dominated mixing layer broadening toward both walls; steep interface gradients persist throughout; flow remains essentially subsonic.

---

## 3. Strategic Methodology

**Exact solution: NOT feasible.** RTI is a genuinely 2D, nonlinear, multi-scale instability: the flow evolves from a perturbed equilibrium through transverse mode growth, shear-driven vortex roll-up, and mixing. The Method of Characteristics applies only to 1D hyperbolic systems / simple waves — it cannot represent transverse instability growth or vortex formation, and no separation-of-variables / Green's-function ansatz exists for the nonlinear Euler system. The Fourier Spectral Method is inapplicable: the $y$-direction has solid (non-periodic) walls, and the solution develops steep interface gradients (Gibbs risk).

**Selection: Discontinuous Galerkin Spectral Element Method in 2D plus time.** It handles wall boundaries naturally, provides local element-wise high order for the smooth vortex cores, and supports AMR + shock capturing for the sharp material interface.

---

## 4. Implementation Requirements (CRITICAL)

* **Configuration:** Use template defaults.

* **3.0.1 Adaptive Mesh Refinement & Shock Capturing — Variable Selection:**
  * **Selected sensor variable: Density $\rho$.**
  * *Signal-to-Noise Analysis:* The structural feature of RTI is the **material interface** (later: spike/bubble boundaries). Density defines this feature sharply and *locally* at all times — a clean factor-2 binary-fluid tracer. In contrast, **velocity** becomes globally chaotic/turbulent in the mixing layer by late time (refining on it wastes elements on noise), and **pressure** is dominated by the smooth hydrostatic background, so its indicator smears over the entire domain instead of localizing at the interface. Density is therefore strictly superior for both the AMR indicator and the shock-capturing indicator for this PDE.

* **3.1 Advisor-Mandated Adjustments (Overrides):** None (Advisor report is empty).

---

## 5. Visualization Strategy

* **Field Selection (Analysis):**
  * **Primary: $\rho$** — the interface/mixing tracer; the driver of the RTI dynamics.
  * **Secondary: $v$** — vertical velocity, showing spike rise, bubble descent, and vortex roll-up.
* **Task:** Plot $\rho$ and $v$ at **4 distinct time steps**: $t = 0.5,\; 1.0,\; 2.0,\; 3.0$ (linear growth → nonlinear onset → roll-up → late mixing).
* **Layout:** Grid — 2 rows (fields: $\rho$, $v$) × 4 columns (times).
* **Saving Data:** Save the **full solution state** (all variables) at `dt_save = 0.1` for Ground Truth. Plotting selection affects only the `summary_all.png` image.
* **Constraints:**
  * Save as: `summary_all.png` (strictly).
  * **CRITICAL: DISABLE COLORBARS (`colorbar=false`)** if the code is in Julia.
  * Do not use fixed colormaps; let scales adjust automatically.
  * Plot only the primitive fields $\rho$ and $v$; do not compute derived quantities.

---

---

- Action: Set \(g=1.0\) in the \(+y\) direction, giving the source \((0,0,g\rho,g\rho v)\), and provide the density accessor required by the shock-capturing and AMR indicators.
- New Code (lines 1-38):
```julia
# ## Problem Setup: Single-Mode Rayleigh–Taylor Instability
#
# Initializes heavy fluid below light fluid across a smoothed interface at
# y = 0.5. A piecewise-hydrostatic pressure and a single-mode vertical
# velocity perturbation seed the instability. Gravity acts in +y.

# %%
@inline function initial_condition_rayleigh_taylor(x, t,
        equations::CompressibleEulerEquations2D)
    x_coord, y = x

    rho = 2.0 + (1.0 - 2.0) * 0.5 *
          (1.0 + tanh(50.0 * (y - 0.5)))
    p = y < 0.5 ? 2.0 * y + 1.0 : y + 1.5

    u = 0.0
    sound_speed = sqrt(equations.gamma * p / rho)
    v = -0.025 * sound_speed * cos(8 * pi * x_coord) * sin(pi * y)^6

    return prim2cons(SVector(rho, u, v, p), equations)
end

@inline function source_terms_gravity(u, x, t,
        equations::CompressibleEulerEquations2D)
    g = 1.0
    rho, rho_v1, rho_v2, rho_e = u

    # Energy source g * rho * v = g * rho_v2.
    return SVector(0.0, 0.0, g * rho, g * rho_v2)
end

@inline function density_indicator(u, equations)
    return u[1]
end
```

```julia
# ## Strategy: Cross-Language Serialization (.npz)
#
# Saves the complete primitive state `(rho, u, v, p)` and the spatial
# coordinates every `dt_save = 0.1` time units for ground-truth data.
# `PlotData2D` reconstructs the adaptive DG solution on a structured grid.

# %%
npz_out = joinpath(@__DIR__, "data_npz")
mkpath(npz_out)

dt_save = 0.1

function save_npz_file(integrator)
    semi = integrator.p
    t = integrator.t

    pd = PlotData2D(integrator.u, semi, solution_variables=cons2prim)

    rho = getindex.(pd.data, 1)
    u = getindex.(pd.data, 2)
    v = getindex.(pd.data, 3)
    p = getindex.(pd.data, 4)

    filename = joinpath(npz_out, Printf.@sprintf("time_%08.5f.npz", t))

    npzwrite(filename, Dict("t" => t, "x" => pd.x, "y" => pd.y,
                            "rho" => rho, "u" => u, "v" => v, "p" => p))
    println("Saved: $(basename(filename))")
    return nothing
end

# Fixed-time saves at t = 0.0, 0.1, ..., 3.0.
save_npz_callback = PeriodicCallback(save_npz_file, dt_save;
                                     initial_affect=true,
                                     save_positions=(false, false))
```

```julia
    for t_plot in plot_times
        filename = joinpath(npz_out,
                            Printf.@sprintf("time_%08.5f.npz", t_plot))
        println(filename)
        data = npzread(filename)

        x = vec(data["x"])
        y = vec(data["y"])
        t_val = data["t"]

        # No clims: each field and snapshot scales automatically.
        push!(plots_array,
              plot(x, y, zcolor=vec(data[field]),
                   title="$(field_label), t=$(round(t_val, digits=2))";
                   style...))
    end
end

println("Saving summary_all.png...")
final_plot = plot(plots_array..., layout=(2, 4),
                  size=(2000, 1200), margin=5Plots.mm)
savefig(final_plot, joinpath(@__DIR__, "summary_all.png"))
println("Done.")
```

Inspector summary:
 The code implements a 2D compressible Euler Rayleigh–Taylor simulation with Trixi DGSEM on a P4estMesh. It uses CompressibleEulerEquations2D(1.4), polydeg=3, domain x in [0,0.25], y in [0,1], tspan [0,3], periodic x and slip walls at y=0,1, the specified tanh density/piecewise pressure/single-mode vertical velocity initial condition, and +y gravity source. Shock capturing is density-based via IndicatorHennemannGassner with Ranocha/Lax-Friedrichs volume fluxes and HLL surface flux; AMR is density-based via IndicatorLöhner with a three-level controller. Time integration is RDPK3SpFSAL49 with StepsizeCallback CFL=0.5. It saves full primitive solution snapshots every dt_save=0.1 and generates summary_all.png with rho and v at t=0.5,1.0,2.0,3.0, colorbar=false.
Proposed name:CompressibleEuler_RayleighTaylor_Periodic_Reflective\Question :Let's solve the 2D Compressible Euler equations for the Rayleigh-Taylor Instability.

**Physics Parameters:**
* **Equations:** Compressible Euler ($\gamma = 1.4$).
* **Gravity:** $g = 1.0$ acting in the **positive $y$-direction** (The source term adds momentum in $+y$).
* **Inviscid:** $\mu = 0$, thermal conductivity $k = 0$.

**Domain:**
$(x,y) \in [0, 0.25] \times [0, 1.0]$
Time interval: $t \in [0, 3.0]$

**Boundary Conditions:**
* $x$-direction: **Periodic**.
* $y$-direction: **Reflective / Free-slip walls** at $y=0$ and $y=1$ (No-penetration condition $v=0$).

**Initial Conditions:**
The domain is split at the interface $y=0.5$.
* **Density ($\rho$):** A smoothed interface between $\rho_{heavy} = 2.0$ (bottom) and $\rho_{light} = 1.0$ (top).
    $$ \rho(y) = \rho_{heavy} + (\rho_{light} - \rho_{heavy}) \cdot \frac{1}{2}\left(1 + \tanh\left(50(y - 0.5)\right)\right) $$
* **Pressure ($p$):** Hydrostatic balance ($\frac{dp}{dy} = \rho g$).
    $$ p(y) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1 & \text{if } y < 0.5 \\ y + 1.5 & \text{if } y \geq 0.5 \end{cases} $$
* **Velocity:** Initialized with zero horizontal velocity ($u=0$) and a perturbation in vertical velocity ($v$):
    $$ v(x,y) = -0.025 \cdot c \cdot \cos(8\pi x) \cdot \sin(\pi y)^6 $$
    Where $c = \sqrt{\gamma p / \rho}$ is the local speed of sound.

  - [Planner]: Instructions received:
Cell 8: Data I/O & Surrogate Interoperability
- Action: `PeriodicCallback` does not guarantee an affect at the terminal time. Add a finalization hook to the same callback so the final integrator state is explicitly serialized through `save_npz_file`, preserving the existing NPZ format and filename convention without requiring changes to Cell 9.
- New Code (lines 31-35):
```julia
# Fixed-time saves at t = 0.0, 0.1, ..., 3.0. PeriodicCallback can skip
# an affect exactly at t_end, so explicitly serialize the final state.
function finalize_npz_save(callback, u, t, integrator)
    if integrator.t >= 3.0 - 8 * eps(3.0)
        save_npz_file(integrator)
    end
    return nothing
end

save_npz_callback = PeriodicCallback(save_npz_file, dt_save;
                                     initial_affect=true,
                                     save_positions=(false, false),
                                     finalize=finalize_npz_save)
```

## Section 1: Status Decision

**Status:** `REJECTED_FOR_REFINEMENT`

The simulation completed successfully and shows a qualitatively plausible Rayleigh–Taylor evolution. However, the figure has a significant rendering artifact, the material interfaces are not demonstrably sharp enough for PINN ground truth, and no convergence or physical-conservation verification is supplied.

The saturation rule is not applicable: there is no trial history and no previous score ≥90.

## Section 2: Quantitative Scorecard

- **Score Integrity:** 43/50
- **Score Accuracy:** 16/20
- **Score Details:** 10/15
- **Score Optimality:** 12/15
- **TOTAL SCORE:** **81/100**

## Section 3: Forensic Analysis & Feedback

### Observation

**Execution and error reporting**

- The log reaches `t=3.0`, saves `time_03.00000.npz`, generates all four requested plot times, and ends with `Done.` There is no reported crash, solver failure, or NaN/Inf diagnostic.
- The requested string **`Minimum achieved error:` is not present in the supplied STDOUT tail**.
- **Quantitative error is not applicable as no ground truth is available.** This is a forward nonlinear RTI simulation, so an error against zero or another artificial reference would not be meaningful.

**Physical evolution**

- At \(t=0.5\), the density interface remains close to \(y=0.5\), with a small perturbation and a localized vertical-velocity response.
- At \(t=1.0\), the central heavy-fluid protrusion is rising in \(+y\), consistent with the initial perturbation and the stated gravity direction.
- At \(t=2.0\), the spike has become strongly nonlinear, with side lobes suggestive of shear-driven roll-up and adjacent light-fluid penetration downward.
- At \(t=3.0\), a long heavy spike and strong vertical-velocity column extend toward the upper wall, with increasingly complex side structures. The evolution is broadly consistent with spike growth, bubble descent, and late-time RTI mixing.
- No gross left–right symmetry violation is apparent. Nevertheless, the spike approaches the upper wall at late time, making verification of the reflective condition \(v=0\) and possible wall interaction particularly important.

**Plot and interface quality**

- Every panel contains dense, regular horizontal white stripes, including otherwise quiescent regions. Because they occur systematically in both fields and at all times, they are more consistent with **mesh-edge/plot-rendering artifacts or missing plot rows** than with physical Gibbs oscillations. They substantially obscure interface sharpness and boundary behavior.
- The visible density fronts are coherent, and there is no clear evidence of severe ringing. However, the spike flanks and roll-up structures appear somewhat soft/pixelated. The figure does not establish that the material interface is resolved within one or two cells.

### Diagnosis

1. **Visualization artifact:** The horizontal striping must be corrected before the solution can be reliably assessed visually. Raw NPZ data should also be checked to exclude missing or non-finite rows.
2. **Potential contact-interface dissipation:** `flux_hll` does not resolve stationary/contact discontinuities as effectively as HLLC. Combined with Lax–Friedrichs shock-capturing blending, it can unnecessarily diffuse the density interface in this predominantly subsonic flow.
3. **Insufficient verification:** Qualitative plausibility alone is not enough for ground-truth generation. Mass conservation, gravitational energy-work balance, positivity, wall behavior, and discretization convergence have not been demonstrated.
4. **Exact-method check:** No exact Method-of-Characteristics, separation-of-variables, or Fourier solution is available for the full nonlinear, two-dimensional, wall-bounded RTI evolution through vortex roll-up and mixing. Linear RTI theory would only describe the early regime. Therefore, DGSEM is an admissible numerical fallback; no exact-method architecture override is warranted.

### Targeted Improvements

- **For Integrity:**
  - Remove element-edge strokes from the plots, e.g. use `edgecolors='none'` and zero line width, or interpolate the DG/AMR solution onto a uniform visualization grid with no gaps between cells.
  - Check every saved NPZ state for finite \(\rho,u,v,p\), positive density and pressure, and missing rows.
  - Add physical axis extents/ticks so that interface position and wall distance can be inspected.

- **For Accuracy:**
  - Add quantitative physical diagnostics: total mass drift, minimum/maximum density and pressure, symmetry error about the expected symmetry plane, wall-normal velocity at \(y=0,1\), and the energy balance
    \[
    \frac{d}{dt}\int E\,dV \approx \int \rho g v\,dV
    \]
    rather than expecting total energy conservation in the presence of gravity.
  - Perform a controlled discretization-convergence comparison at the saved output times. Use **`polydeg=4`** for the next run; `polydeg=5` is preferable to further aggressive global mesh refinement if cost and stability remain acceptable. Reduce CFL to approximately 0.3–0.4 only if the higher-degree run requires it.

- **For Details:**
  - Replace **`flux_hll` with `flux_hllc`** while retaining `flux_ranocha` in the volume. This should reduce dissipation at the material/contact interface.
  - Keep density-based AMR, ensuring the spike and bubble boundaries reach the permitted refinement level. Avoid further global refinement unless the convergence test demonstrates that it is necessary.
  - Because strong shocks are not expected, reduce the aggressiveness of the Lax–Friedrichs blending—through `alpha_max` or its activation threshold—so it acts only where genuinely needed. Retain a local stabilization/positivity safeguard if HLLC or higher polynomial degree produces interface oscillations.

- **For Optimality:**
  - Retain DGSEM, density-based AMR, the Ranocha volume flux, and the adaptive explicit time integrator. The principal methodological upgrade is **HLLC plus more selective shock capturing**, not a change of PDE solver architecture.

--- Strategy Report Received ---
# ATHENA Lab — Scientific Strategy Report
**Problem:** 2D Compressible Euler — Rayleigh–Taylor Instability (Refinement Run 3)
**Verdict:** Retain DGSEM architecture; execute Advisor-mandated upgrades (HLLC, polydeg=4, selective shock capturing, artifact-free rendering, full physical verification suite).

---

**1. Problem Statement**

Forward simulation of the single-mode compressible Rayleigh–Taylor instability: inviscid Euler equations (γ = 1.4) with a gravitational source (g = 1.0 in +y) on $[0,0.25]\times[0,1]$, $t\in[0,3]$. Heavy fluid (ρ = 2) lies beneath light fluid (ρ = 1) across a smoothed interface at y = 0.5; since gravity points toward the light fluid, the configuration is unstable. A single-wavelength perturbation seeds a central heavy spike rising in +y. Objective: a ground-truth-quality dataset of the full nonlinear evolution (linear growth → spike/bubble → KH roll-up → late-time mixing near the top wall).

This is a **refinement run** of the previous attempt (81/100). The Advisor's diagnosis mandates: (i) eliminate the horizontal white-stripe rendering artifact; (ii) replace the contact-diffusing HLL flux with HLLC; (iii) raise resolution to polydeg = 4; (iv) make Lax–Friedrichs blending selective; (v) supply quantitative physical verification (mass, positivity, symmetry, wall compliance, energy balance).

---

**2. Mathematical Specification (Code-Ready Extraction)**

* **Governing Equations:** 2D compressible Euler with gravity source:
$$\partial_t \begin{pmatrix} \rho \\ \rho u \\ \rho v \\ E \end{pmatrix} + \partial_x \begin{pmatrix} \rho u \\ \rho u^2 + p \\ \rho u v \\ u(E+p) \end{pmatrix} + \partial_y \begin{pmatrix} \rho v \\ \rho u v \\ \rho v^2 + p \\ v(E+p) \end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix} 0 \\ 0 \\ \rho g \\ \rho g v \end{pmatrix}$$
with total energy $E = \dfrac{p}{\gamma-1} + \dfrac{1}{2}\rho(u^2+v^2)$. The energy source is the gravity power $S_E = \rho g v$ (force × velocity — **not** $\rho g v^2$).

* **Domain:** $(x,y) \in [0, 0.25] \times [0, 1.0]$; $t \in [0, 3.0]$.

* **Boundary Conditions:** x: **Periodic**. y = 0 and y = 1: **Reflective / free-slip walls** (no-penetration $v = 0$, zero normal flux).

* **Initial Conditions** (interface at $y_i = 0.5$):
$$\rho(x,y,0) = 2.0 + (1.0 - 2.0)\cdot\frac{1}{2}\Big(1 + \tanh\big(50\,(y - 0.5)\big)\Big)$$
$$p(y,0) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1, & y < 0.5 \\ y + 1.5, & y \geq 0.5 \end{cases} \qquad (\text{hydrostatic } dp/dy = \rho g \text{ away from the interface})$$
$$u(x,y,0) = 0$$
$$v(x,y,0) = -0.025\; c(x,y)\, \cos(8\pi x)\, \big(\sin(\pi y)\big)^{6}, \qquad c(x,y) = \sqrt{\frac{\gamma\, p(y)}{\rho(y)}}$$
$$E(x,y,0) = \frac{p}{\gamma - 1} + \frac{1}{2}\rho\left(u^2 + v^2\right) \quad (\text{via prim2cons})$$

* **Constants & Parameters:** $\gamma = 1.4$; $g = 1.0$; gravity vector $\mathbf{g} = [0, 1.0]^T$; $\rho_{heavy} = 2.0$; $\rho_{light} = 1.0$; interface $y = 0.5$; tanh steepness $50$; perturbation amplitude $0.025$; wavenumber $8\pi$ (one full wavelength across the domain: $8\pi \cdot 0.25 = 2\pi$); $\mu = 0$, $k = 0$.

* **Expected Physics:** Exponential single-mode RTI growth ($t \lesssim 1$); nonlinear heavy spike rising in +y from $x \approx 0.125$ with a descending light bubble ($t \approx 1$–$2$); Kelvin–Helmholtz roll-up on the spike flanks ($t \approx 2$–$3$); late-time mixing with the spike approaching the top wall $y=1$ by $t = 3$ (reflective wall interaction). Predominantly subsonic flow with at most weak shocklets — the dominant features are **contact/material interfaces**. The solution is mirror-symmetric about $x = 0.125$.

---

**3. Strategic Methodology**

* **Exact solution feasibility: NO.** The full nonlinear, wall-bounded, two-dimensional RTI evolution through vortex roll-up and mixing admits no closed-form solution. MoC applies only to 1D hyperbolic systems/simple waves — inapplicable to multi-dimensional nonlinear evolution with source terms, contact discontinuities, and slip walls. Linear RTI theory covers only the early incompressible regime. Fourier spectral methods are inapplicable (non-periodic reflective walls in y; discontinuity formation → Gibbs). The Advisor concurs: no exact-method architecture override is warranted.
* **Selection:** **DGSEM in 2D plus time** — high-order, conservative, AMR-capable, native handling of periodic + slip-wall BCs, entropy-stable volume fluxes, positivity-friendly shock capturing. Architecture retained per Advisor; the upgrade is *flux physics + selectivity + verification*, not a solver change.

---

**4. Implementation Requirements (CRITICAL)**

* **Configuration:** Use template defaults, **EXCEPT** the Advisor-mandated overrides in §4.1. Do not change the time integrator, AMR controller levels, or base mesh otherwise.

* **4.0.1 Adaptive Mesh Refinement & Shock Capturing — Variable Selection Analysis:**
  * **AMR variable: Density (ρ).** The structural features of RTI — material interface, rising heavy spike, descending light bubble, KH roll-up cores — are *density* discontinuities. ρ stays bounded (≈ 1–2 with mild compressible excursions) and has steep gradients **only** at these features, so density-based refinement tags exactly the dynamically relevant structures. Velocity (especially $v$) becomes globally chaotic across the entire mixing layer at late times — refining on it wastes resolution on turbulence rather than interfaces. Pressure carries smooth acoustic/hydrostatic fluctuations everywhere, which would trigger spurious global refinement. Density is also the buoyancy driver of the instability, making it the physically correct sensor. → IndicatorLöhner on ρ, ControllerThreeLevel (base 4, med 5, max 6); the spike/bubble boundaries must reach the maximum refinement level.
  * **Shock capturing:** retain the hybrid blending mechanism, made selective per §4.1.3.

* **4.1 Advisor-Mandated Adjustments (Overrides — supersede template defaults):**
  1. **polydeg = 4** (up from 3). No further global mesh refinement.
  2. **Surface flux: `flux_hllc`** (replaces `flux_hll`); **volume flux: `flux_ranocha` (retained)**. HLLC restores the contact-resolution property required for a sharp material interface in this subsonic flow.
  3. **Selective shock capturing:** reduce the Lax–Friedrichs blending aggressiveness — lower the blending cap `alpha_max` to ≈ 0.2 (from the 0.5 default) and/or raise the activation threshold (`alpha_min`) so blending engages only in genuinely non-smooth elements. Strong shocks are not expected. Retain the local stabilization/positivity safeguard against any interface oscillations introduced by HLLC or the higher polynomial degree.
  4. **CFL = 0.4** (within the Advisor-sanctioned 0.3–0.4 band) as a stability safeguard for the polydeg = 4 run; fall back to 0.3 only if instability/NaN is detected.
  5. **Visualization artifact fix (mandatory):** eliminate the horizontal white stripes. Interpolate the DG/AMR solution onto a **uniform, gap-free visualization grid** (≥ 8–16 points per element per direction, every AMR leaf cell covered contiguously, every grid point assigned exactly one value) and render with **no element-edge strokes** (`edgecolors='none'`, zero line width, or nearest/gouraud shading). Add physical axis extents and ticks ($x \in [0,0.25]$, $y \in [0,1]$) so interface position and wall distance are inspectable.
  6. **Physical diagnostics (mandatory — print to STDOUT at every save interval and at final time):**
     * Total mass drift: $\Delta M(t) = \int \rho\, dV - M(0)$ (should remain near machine zero).
     * Positivity: min/max of ρ and p (require ρ > 0, p > 0 everywhere).
     * Symmetry error about the expected symmetry plane $x = 0.125$: $\epsilon_{sym} = \|\rho(x,y) - \rho(0.25 - x, y)\|_2 / \|\rho\|_2$.
     * Slip-wall compliance: max $|v|$ along $y = 0$ and $y = 1$.
     * Energy balance with gravity: $\frac{d}{dt}\int E\, dV$ (finite difference between saves) vs. $\int \rho g v\, dV$ (instantaneous gravity work). Total energy is **not** conserved in the presence of the source; agreement of the two sides validates the source implementation.
  7. **NPZ sanity check (mandatory):** after every save, verify the stored state is finite in ρ, u, v, p, strictly positive in ρ and p, and has complete coverage (no missing rows / NaN gaps).
  8. **Verification output:** print a final summary line beginning exactly with `Minimum achieved error:` reporting the smallest relative discrepancy achieved among the verification metrics (mass drift, symmetry error, wall-velocity violation, energy-balance closure, and — if the archived polydeg = 3 NPZ states remain on disk — the discretization-convergence difference $\|\rho_{p4} - \rho_{p3}\|_2 / \|\rho_{p3}\|_2$ at common save times). Do not fabricate an artificial ground-truth error.

---

**5. Visualization Strategy**

* **Field Selection:** plot **ρ (density)** and **v (vertical velocity)** only. Density is the primary driver (buoyancy from the density contrast) and directly displays interface, spike, bubble, and roll-up; vertical velocity captures the dominant dynamics (rising spike column, descending bubble, roll-up circulation). Do not plot u, p, E, or derived quantities.
* **Times:** 4 snapshots at $t = 0.5,\ 1.0,\ 2.0,\ 3.0$.
* **Layout:** grid — 2 rows (ρ, v) × 4 columns (times).
* **Rendering:** uniform-grid interpolation with no element edges (per §4.1.5); automatic per-panel color scaling (no fixed limits/colormaps); physical axes with ticks.
* **CRITICAL (Julia):** `colorbar = false` — colorbars MUST be disabled.
* **Saving Data:** save the **full solution state** (all variables) every `dt_save = 0.1` for Ground Truth.
* **Save as:** `summary_all.png` (strictly).

---

**6. Cross-Validation & Data Integration (Conditional)**

* **Trigger check:** [USER_QUESTION] contains no external data paths (no sensors, no reference solution) → external cross-validation not applicable.
* **Internal validation:** the diagnostics of §4.1.6–4.1.8 constitute the quantitative verification suite. If the previous run's NPZ archives (dt_save = 0.1, polydeg = 3) are accessible, additionally compute and print the relative $L_2$ difference of ρ (and the spike-tip position) between the polydeg = 3 and polydeg = 4 solutions at common output times as the controlled discretization-convergence comparison.

- New Code (lines 7-36):
```julia
npz_out = joinpath(@__DIR__, "data_npz")
mkpath(npz_out)
dt_save = 0.1

# Verification state is retained across callback invocations. The final
# summary cell uses verification_minima without inventing a ground truth.
initial_total_mass = Ref(NaN)
previous_energy_time = Ref(NaN)
previous_total_energy = Ref(NaN)
last_npz_save_time = Ref(NaN)
verification_minima = Dict{Symbol, Float64}()

@inline diagnostic_mass(u, equations) = u[1]
@inline diagnostic_total_energy(u, equations) = u[4]
# g = 1 and rho * g * v = g * rho_v2.
@inline diagnostic_gravity_work(u, equations) = u[3]

function record_verification_error!(name, value)
    if isfinite(value)
        verification_minima[name] =
            min(get(verification_minima, name, Inf), abs(value))
    end
    return nothing
end

function verify_npz_file(filename)
    data = npzread(filename)
    for key in ("t", "x", "y", "rho", "u", "v", "p")
        haskey(data, key) || error("NPZ sanity check: missing key $key in $filename")
    end

    n = length(data["rho"])
    complete_coverage = n > 0 &&
                        length(data["u"]) == n &&
                        length(data["v"]) == n &&
                        length(data["p"]) == n &&
                        length(data["x"]) == n &&
                        length(data["y"]) == n

    finite_state = all(key -> all(isfinite, data[key]),
                       ("rho", "u", "v", "p"))
    finite_coordinates = all(isfinite, data["x"]) &&
                         all(isfinite, data["y"])
    positive_state = all(value -> value > 0, data["rho"]) &&
                     all(value -> value > 0, data["p"])

    if !(complete_coverage && finite_state && finite_coordinates &&
         positive_state)
        error("NPZ sanity check failed for $filename: " *
              "coverage=$complete_coverage, finite_state=$finite_state, " *
              "finite_coordinates=$finite_coordinates, " *
              "positive_rho_p=$positive_state")
    end
    return nothing
end

function mirror_symmetry_error(x, y, rho)
    # Pair DG/plot nodes with their images under x -> 0.25 - x.
    # Rounding only supplies a tolerant dictionary key for coordinates
    # generated independently on opposite sides of the symmetric mesh.
    node_lookup = Dict{Tuple{Float64, Float64}, Int}()
    for k in eachindex(x)
        key = (round(x[k], digits=10), round(y[k], digits=10))
        node_lookup[key] = k
    end

    numerator = 0.0
    denominator = 0.0
    matched_nodes = 0
    for k in eachindex(x)
        mirror_key = (round(0.25 - x[k], digits=10),
                      round(y[k], digits=10))
        if haskey(node_lookup, mirror_key)
            mirror_k = node_lookup[mirror_key]
            difference = rho[k] - rho[mirror_k]
            numerator += difference^2
            denominator += rho[k]^2
            matched_nodes += 1
        end
    end

    if matched_nodes == 0 || denominator == 0
        return NaN
    end
    return sqrt(numerator / denominator)
end

function print_physical_diagnostics!(integrator, pd, rho, v, p)
    semi = integrator.p
    t = integrator.t

    # Use the DG quadrature for integral quantities rather than treating
    # nonuniformly spaced visualization nodes as a uniform grid.
    total_mass = Trixi.integrate(diagnostic_mass, integrator.u, semi)
    total_energy = Trixi.integrate(diagnostic_total_energy, integrator.u, semi)
    gravity_work = Trixi.integrate(diagnostic_gravity_work, integrator.u, semi)

    if !isfinite(initial_total_mass[])
        initial_total_mass[] = total_mass
    end
    mass_drift = total_mass - initial_total_mass[]
    relative_mass_drift = abs(mass_drift) /
                          max(abs(initial_total_mass[]), eps(Float64))

    x = vec(pd.x)
    y = vec(pd.y)
    rho_values = vec(rho)
    v_values = vec(v)
    p_values = vec(p)

    rho_min, rho_max = extrema(rho_values)
    p_min, p_max = extrema(p_values)
    symmetry_error = mirror_symmetry_error(x, y, rho_values)

    wall_velocity = 0.0
    max_abs_v = 0.0
    wall_samples = 0
    wall_tolerance = 1.0e-10
    for k in eachindex(y)
        max_abs_v = max(max_abs_v, abs(v_values[k]))
        if abs(y[k]) <= wall_tolerance ||
           abs(y[k] - 1.0) <= wall_tolerance
            wall_velocity = max(wall_velocity, abs(v_values[k]))
            wall_samples += 1
        end
    end
    if wall_samples == 0
        wall_velocity = NaN
        relative_wall_violation = NaN
    else
        relative_wall_violation = wall_velocity /
                                  max(max_abs_v, eps(Float64))
    end

    if isfinite(previous_energy_time[]) && t > previous_energy_time[]
        dE_dt = (total_energy - previous_total_energy[]) /
                (t - previous_energy_time[])
        energy_closure = abs(dE_dt - gravity_work) /
                         max(abs(dE_dt), abs(gravity_work), eps(Float64))
    else
        dE_dt = NaN
        energy_closure = NaN
    end

    record_verification_error!(:mass, relative_mass_drift)
    record_verification_error!(:symmetry, symmetry_error)
    record_verification_error!(:wall, relative_wall_violation)
    record_verification_error!(:energy_balance, energy_closure)

    println(Printf.@sprintf(
        "Diagnostics t=%.5f: dM=%.6e, rel_dM=%.6e, " *
        "rho=[%.6e,%.6e], p=[%.6e,%.6e], sym=%.6e, " *
        "wall_max_abs_v=%.6e, dE_dt=%.6e, gravity_work=%.6e, " *
        "energy_closure=%.6e",
        t, mass_drift, relative_mass_drift,
        rho_min, rho_max, p_min, p_max, symmetry_error,
        wall_velocity, dE_dt, gravity_work, energy_closure))

    previous_energy_time[] = t
    previous_total_energy[] = total_energy
    return nothing
end

function save_npz_file(integrator)
    semi = integrator.p
    t = integrator.t

    # PeriodicCallback and its finalizer can both reach t_end.
    if isfinite(last_npz_save_time[]) &&
       abs(t - last_npz_save_time[]) <= 1.0e-10
        return nothing
    end

    pd = PlotData2D(integrator.u, semi, solution_variables=cons2prim)
    rho = getindex.(pd.data, 1)
    u = getindex.(pd.data, 2)
    v = getindex.(pd.data, 3)
    p = getindex.(pd.data, 4)
    filename = joinpath(npz_out, Printf.@sprintf("time_%08.5f.npz", t))
    npzwrite(filename, Dict("t" => t, "x" => pd.x, "y" => pd.y,
                            "rho" => rho, "u" => u, "v" => v, "p" => p))

    # Re-read the actual archive so the check covers serialization as well
    # as the in-memory PlotData2D reconstruction.
    verify_npz_file(filename)
    println("Saved and NPZ-verified: $(basename(filename))")
    print_physical_diagnostics!(integrator, pd, rho, v, p)

    last_npz_save_time[] = t
    return nothing
end

# Fixed-time saves at t = 0.0, 0.1, ..., 3.0. PeriodicCallback can skip
# an affect exactly at t_end, so explicitly serialize the final state.
function finalize_npz_save(callback, u, t, integrator)
    if integrator.t >= 3.0 - 8 * eps(3.0)
        save_npz_file(integrator)
    end
    return nothing
end

save_npz_callback = PeriodicCallback(save_npz_file, dt_save;
                                     initial_affect=true,
                                     save_positions=(false, false),
                                     finalize=finalize_npz_save)
```

  - [Planner]: Instructions received:
Cell 11: Data Regularization (Rasterization)
- Action: Replace NaN-producing point binning with complete-coverage reconstruction on a cell-centered uniform grid. The default `512 × 512` grid provides eight visualization samples per finest level-6 AMR element in each direction.
- Action: Seed each occupied pixel with the nearest source sample, then fill unoccupied pixels by nearest-neighbor propagation so every pixel receives exactly one finite value and no transparent rows remain.
- New Code (lines 19-39):
```julia
function rasterize_data(x, y, z, nx=512, ny=512)
    x_min, x_max = 0.0, 0.25
    y_min, y_max = 0.0, 1.0
    nx > 0 && ny > 0 || error("Visualization grid dimensions must be positive")

    x_source = Float64.(vec(x))
    y_source = Float64.(vec(y))
    z_source = Float64.(vec(z))
    n_source = length(z_source)

    n_source > 0 || error("Cannot rasterize an empty solution")
    length(x_source) == n_source || error("x/z data lengths do not match")
    length(y_source) == n_source || error("y/z data lengths do not match")
    all(isfinite, x_source) && all(isfinite, y_source) &&
        all(isfinite, z_source) ||
        error("Cannot rasterize non-finite solution data")

    dx = (x_max - x_min) / nx
    dy = (y_max - y_min) / ny

    # There are no NaN padding rows: every pixel is filled below.
    grid = fill(NaN, ny, nx)
    nearest_distance = fill(Inf, ny, nx)

    # Seed occupied pixels with the source node nearest to each pixel center.
    # If several nodes occupy one pixel, this avoids order-dependent overwrites.
    for k in 1:n_source
        i = clamp(floor(Int, (x_source[k] - x_min) / dx) + 1, 1, nx)
        j = clamp(floor(Int, (y_source[k] - y_min) / dy) + 1, 1, ny)
        x_center = x_min + (i - 0.5) * dx
        y_center = y_min + (j - 0.5) * dy
        distance = (x_source[k] - x_center)^2 +
                   (y_source[k] - y_center)^2

        if distance < nearest_distance[j, i]
            nearest_distance[j, i] = distance
            grid[j, i] = z_source[k]
        end
    end

    # Multi-source nearest-neighbor propagation supplies an interpolated value
    # to pixels between DG visualization nodes, including every AMR leaf-cell
    # region. Each previously empty pixel is assigned exactly once.
    queue_i = Int[]
    queue_j = Int[]
    sizehint!(queue_i, nx * ny)
    sizehint!(queue_j, nx * ny)

    for j in 1:ny, i in 1:nx
        if !isnan(grid[j, i])
            push!(queue_i, i)
            push!(queue_j, j)
        end
    end

    head = 1
    while head <= length(queue_i)
        i = queue_i[head]
        j = queue_j[head]
        head += 1
        value = grid[j, i]

        for (di, dj) in ((-1, 0), (1, 0), (0, -1), (0, 1))
            neighbor_i = i + di
            neighbor_j = j + dj
            if 1 <= neighbor_i <= nx && 1 <= neighbor_j <= ny &&
               isnan(grid[neighbor_j, neighbor_i])
                grid[neighbor_j, neighbor_i] = value
                push!(queue_i, neighbor_i)
                push!(queue_j, neighbor_j)
            end
        end
    end

    all(isfinite, grid) ||
        error("Uniform visualization grid reconstruction left gaps")
    return grid
end
```

```julia
println("Generating Rayleigh–Taylor summary plots...")
plots_array = []
plot_times = (0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 3.0)

# Eight samples per finest level-6 AMR element in each direction.
vis_nx, vis_ny = 512, 512
vis_x = range(0.25 / (2 * vis_nx),
              0.25 - 0.25 / (2 * vis_nx); length=vis_nx)
vis_y = range(1.0 / (2 * vis_ny),
              1.0 - 1.0 / (2 * vis_ny); length=vis_ny)

style = (
    aspect_ratio = :equal,
    xlims = (0.0, 0.25),
    ylims = (0.0, 1.0),
    xticks = ([0.0, 0.125, 0.25], ["0", "0.125", "0.25"]),
    yticks = 0.0:0.2:1.0,
    xlabel = "x",
    ylabel = "y",
    framestyle = :box,
    grid = false,
    legend = false,
    colorbar = false,
    linewidth = 0,       # No element-edge strokes.
    margin = 2Plots.mm
)

# Field-major ordering: four density panels, then four vertical-velocity panels.
for (field, field_label) in (("rho", "ρ"), ("v", "v"))
    for t_plot in plot_times
        filename = joinpath(npz_out,
                            Printf.@sprintf("time_%08.5f.npz", t_plot))
        println(filename)
        verify_npz_file(filename)
        data = npzread(filename)

        # No clims: every field and snapshot receives automatic color scaling.
        field_grid = rasterize_data(data["x"], data["y"], data[field],
                                    vis_nx, vis_ny)
        t_val = data["t"]
        push!(plots_array,
              heatmap(vis_x, vis_y, field_grid;
                      title="$(field_label), t=$(round(t_val, digits=2))",
                      style...))
    end
end

println("Saving summary_all.png...")
final_plot = plot(plots_array..., layout=(2, 4),
                  size=(2000, 1200), margin=5Plots.mm)
savefig(final_plot, joinpath(@__DIR__, "summary_all.png"))

# The heavy/light threshold is the midpoint of the initial density contrast.
function spike_tip_position(data)
    rho = vec(data["rho"])
    y = vec(data["y"])
    heavy_nodes = rho .>= 1.5
    return any(heavy_nodes) ? maximum(y[heavy_nodes]) : NaN
end

function compare_polydeg3_archives!()
    archive_directories = String[]

    # Never interpret the newly written polydeg=4 directory as an archive.
    for path in readdir(@__DIR__; join=true)
        name = lowercase(basename(path))
        if isdir(path) && abspath(path) != abspath(npz_out) &&
           ((occursin("polydeg", name) && occursin("3", name)) ||
            occursin("p3", name))
            push!(archive_directories, path)
        end
    end

    for archive_directory in archive_directories
        compared = false
        for save_index in 0:round(Int, 3.0 / dt_save)
            t = save_index * dt_save
            filename = Printf.@sprintf("time_%08.5f.npz", t)
            p4_filename = joinpath(npz_out, filename)
            p3_filename = joinpath(archive_directory, filename)

            if isfile(p4_filename) && isfile(p3_filename)
                p4_data = npzread(p4_filename)
                p3_data = npzread(p3_filename)
                required_keys = ("x", "y", "rho")
                if !all(key -> haskey(p4_data, key) && haskey(p3_data, key),
                        required_keys)
                    continue
                end

                # Compare both solutions on the same gap-free lattice.
                rho_p4 = rasterize_data(p4_data["x"], p4_data["y"],
                                        p4_data["rho"], 256, 256)
                rho_p3 = rasterize_data(p3_data["x"], p3_data["y"],
                                        p3_data["rho"], 256, 256)
                denominator = sqrt(sum(abs2, rho_p3))

                if denominator > 0
                    discrepancy = sqrt(sum(abs2, rho_p4 - rho_p3)) /
                                  denominator
                    record_verification_error!(:discretization_convergence,
                                               discrepancy)

                    tip_p4 = spike_tip_position(p4_data)
                    tip_p3 = spike_tip_position(p3_data)
                    println(Printf.@sprintf(
                        "Discretization comparison t=%.5f: " *
                        "relative_rho_L2=%.6e, spike_tip_p4=%.6e, " *
                        "spike_tip_p3=%.6e, abs_tip_difference=%.6e",
                        t, discrepancy, tip_p4, tip_p3,
                        abs(tip_p4 - tip_p3)))
                    compared = true
                end
            end
        end

        if compared
            return nothing
        end
    end

    println("No accessible archived polydeg=3 NPZ states with common save times; " *
            "skipping discretization-convergence comparison.")
    return nothing
end

function print_verification_summary()
    best_metric = :none
    best_value = Inf

    for (metric, value) in verification_minima
        if isfinite(value) && value < best_value
            best_metric = metric
            best_value = value
        end
    end

    if isfinite(best_value)
        println("Minimum achieved error: ",
                Printf.@sprintf("%.6e", best_value),
                " (", best_metric, "); verification minima=",
                verification_minima)
    else
        println("Minimum achieved error: unavailable " *
                "(no finite verification metrics)")
    end
    return nothing
end

compare_polydeg3_archives!()
println("Done.")
print_verification_summary()
```

Proposed name:CompressibleEuler_RayleighTaylor_Periodic_Reflective\Question :Let's solve the 2D Compressible Euler equations for the Rayleigh-Taylor Instability.

**Physics Parameters:**
* **Equations:** Compressible Euler ($\gamma = 1.4$).
* **Gravity:** $g = 1.0$ acting in the **positive $y$-direction** (The source term adds momentum in $+y$).
* **Inviscid:** $\mu = 0$, thermal conductivity $k = 0$.

**Domain:**
$(x,y) \in [0, 0.25] \times [0, 1.0]$
Time interval: $t \in [0, 3.0]$

**Boundary Conditions:**
* $x$-direction: **Periodic**.
* $y$-direction: **Reflective / Free-slip walls** at $y=0$ and $y=1$ (No-penetration condition $v=0$).

**Initial Conditions:**
The domain is split at the interface $y=0.5$.
* **Density ($\rho$):** A smoothed interface between $\rho_{heavy} = 2.0$ (bottom) and $\rho_{light} = 1.0$ (top).
    $$ \rho(y) = \rho_{heavy} + (\rho_{light} - \rho_{heavy}) \cdot \frac{1}{2}\left(1 + \tanh\left(50(y - 0.5)\right)\right) $$
* **Pressure ($p$):** Hydrostatic balance ($\frac{dp}{dy} = \rho g$).
    $$ p(y) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1 & \text{if } y < 0.5 \\ y + 1.5 & \text{if } y \geq 0.5 \end{cases} $$
* **Velocity:** Initialized with zero horizontal velocity ($u=0$) and a perturbation in vertical velocity ($v$):
    $$ v(x,y) = -0.025 \cdot c \cdot \cos(8\pi x) \cdot \sin(\pi y)^6 $$
    Where $c = \sqrt{\gamma p / \rho}$ is the local speed of sound.

--- 4. Calling Debugging Team ---
Inspector summary:
 The code attempts to implement the 2D compressible Euler Rayleigh–Taylor instability in Trixi with DGSEM. It sets gamma=1.4, domain [0,0.25] x [0,1], t in [0,3], the specified smoothed density interface, piecewise hydrostatic pressure, single-mode vertical velocity perturbation, and gravity source (0,0,rho,rho*v). Numerics use polydeg=4, HLLC surface flux, Ranocha volume flux, selective Hennemann–Gassner shock capturing with alpha_max=0.2/alpha_min=0.01, density-based Lohner AMR with ControllerThreeLevel base=4/med=5/max=6, and CFL=0.4. The code also includes diagnostics, NPZ saving, plotting, and conditional polydeg=3 comparison logic. However, the custom NPZ field extraction is structurally wrong, so the saved data and downstream diagnostics/plots do not match the required full-state output.
Report: There are discrepancies with the base strategy:
Critical data-extraction mismatch: `rho = getindex.(pd.data, 1)` and the analogous calls for `u`, `v`, and `p` do not extract the full DG/AMR fields. Because `pd.data` is a vector of per-variable arrays, broadcasting `getindex(..., 1)` returns the first element of each variable array, producing length-4 vectors instead of the density, x-velocity, y-velocity, and pressure fields. Therefore the NPZ keys `rho`, `u`, `v`, `p` are not full solution states, the mandatory NPZ sanity check cannot pass, and the verification diagnostics and `summary_all.png` are computed from invalid arrays. The strategy requires saving the full solution state every dt_save=0.1 and verifying it after every save, so this violation rejects the implementation. The intended extraction should index the variable arrays directly (e.g. `pd.data[1]`, `pd.data[2]`, `pd.data[3]`, `pd.data[4]` or equivalent named access).

Inspector summary:
 The code implements the 2D compressible Euler Rayleigh–Taylor instability using Trixi DGSEM with polydeg=4, flux_hllc surface flux, flux_ranocha volume flux, and selective hybrid shock capturing using flux_lax_friedrichs with alpha_max=0.2 and alpha_min=0.01. The mesh is P4est on [0,0.25]×[0,1] with periodic x and slip walls at y=0 and y=1. AMR uses IndicatorLöhner on density with ControllerThreeLevel base level 4, medium level 5, max level 6. The initial condition matches the strategy: smoothed density interface at y=0.5, piecewise hydrostatic pressure, u=0, v=-0.025 c cos(8πx) sin(πy)^6, and conservative energy via prim2cons. Gravity source adds ρg to y-momentum and ρgv to energy. Time span is [0,3] with StepsizeCallback CFL=0.4. The code includes mandatory diagnostics for mass drift, positivity, symmetry about x=0.125, slip-wall compliance, and gravity energy balance, plus NPZ sanity checks, a final 'Minimum achieved error:' summary, and optional polydeg=3 archive comparison. Visualization saves summary_all.png with only ρ and v at t=0.5,1.0,2.0,3.0 in a 2×4 layout, with no colorbars, no element edges, physical axes, and uniform 512×512 rasterization.
Proposed name:CompressibleEuler_RayleighTaylor_Periodic_Reflective\Question :Let's solve the 2D Compressible Euler equations for the Rayleigh-Taylor Instability.

**Physics Parameters:**
* **Equations:** Compressible Euler ($\gamma = 1.4$).
* **Gravity:** $g = 1.0$ acting in the **positive $y$-direction** (The source term adds momentum in $+y$).
* **Inviscid:** $\mu = 0$, thermal conductivity $k = 0$.

**Domain:**
$(x,y) \in [0, 0.25] \times [0, 1.0]$
Time interval: $t \in [0, 3.0]$

**Boundary Conditions:**
* $x$-direction: **Periodic**.
* $y$-direction: **Reflective / Free-slip walls** at $y=0$ and $y=1$ (No-penetration condition $v=0$).

**Initial Conditions:**
The domain is split at the interface $y=0.5$.
* **Density ($\rho$):** A smoothed interface between $\rho_{heavy} = 2.0$ (bottom) and $\rho_{light} = 1.0$ (top).
    $$ \rho(y) = \rho_{heavy} + (\rho_{light} - \rho_{heavy}) \cdot \frac{1}{2}\left(1 + \tanh\left(50(y - 0.5)\right)\right) $$
* **Pressure ($p$):** Hydrostatic balance ($\frac{dp}{dy} = \rho g$).
    $$ p(y) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1 & \text{if } y < 0.5 \\ y + 1.5 & \text{if } y \geq 0.5 \end{cases} $$
* **Velocity:** Initialized with zero horizontal velocity ($u=0$) and a perturbation in vertical velocity ($v$):
    $$ v(x,y) = -0.025 \cdot c \cdot \cos(8\pi x) \cdot \sin(\pi y)^6 $$
    Where $c = \sqrt{\gamma p / \rho}$ is the local speed of sound.

**Output:**
Create an appropiate Figure and saved it as summary_all.png

Inspector summary:
 The code implements a 2D DGSEM (method-of-lines) solver for the single-mode compressible Rayleigh–Taylor instability using Trixi.jl on a P4estMesh over [0,0.25]×[0,1] with t∈[0,3]. Physics matches the strategy exactly: CompressibleEulerEquations2D(γ=1.4); density profile ρ=2+(1-2)*0.5*(1+tanh(50(y-0.5))); piecewise hydrostatic pressure p=2y+1 (y<0.5)/y+1.5 (y≥0.5); u=0; v=-0.025·c·cos(8πx)·sin(πy)^6 with c=sqrt(γp/ρ); and total energy via prim2cons. The gravity source returns (0,0,ρg,ρgv) — correctly ρgv (not ρgv²) for the energy source. Configuration follows most Advisor overrides: polydeg=4 (LobattoLegendreBasis), surface_flux=flux_hllc, volume_flux_dg=flux_ranocha, volume_flux_fv=flux_lax_friedrichs via VolumeIntegralShockCapturingHG, CFL=0.4 (StepsizeCallback), density-based AMR using IndicatorLöhner(variable=density) with ControllerThreeLevel(base=4, med=5, max=6) at interval=20, periodic-x / slip-wall-y boundaries, and RDPK3SpFSAL49 with adaptive=false. A full verification suite is present (mass drift via Trixi.integrate, min/max ρ and p, mirror-symmetry error about x=0.125, wall |v| compliance, energy-balance dE/dt vs ∫ρgv, NPZ sanity check, optional polydeg=3 convergence comparison, and a final line beginning 'Minimum achieved error:'). Visualization produces summary_all.png as a 2×4 grid of ρ and v at t=0.5,1.0,2.0,3.0 using a gap-free uniform rasterization with no element edges and colorbar=false.
Report: There are discrepancies with the base strategy:
Shock-capturing configuration violates the selective-blending mandate of §4.1.3. Although alpha_max=0.2 is correctly set, the indicator also sets alpha_min=0.5. In Trixi's IndicatorHennemannGassner, alpha_min is the *floor* of the blending coefficient (default ≈1e-3), so alpha_min=0.5 forces a 50% Lax–Friedrichs blending in smooth elements and, combined with alpha_max=0.2, yields an inverted range (alpha_min > alpha_max). Either the linear map alpha=alpha_min+(alpha_max-alpha_min)·ζ makes smooth regions blend MORE (~0.5) than non-smooth ones (~0.2), or a clamp effectively applies ~0.2 blending uniformly. In both interpretations the strategy's explicit goal — that 'blending engages only in genuinely non-smooth elements' — is not achieved; substantial artificial dissipation is applied everywhere, negating the HLLC contact-resolution improvement and the reduced-aggressiveness intent of this refinement run. (A correct implementation would keep alpha_min near its small default, e.g. ~1e-3, so blending switches off in smooth regions.) All other required elements — ICs, source term (ρgv), polydeg=4, flux_hllc, flux_ranocha, CFL=0.4, density-based AMR levels, the diagnostics/NPZ/summary deliverables — match the strategy.

Inspector summary:
 Numerical simulation using Trixi's DGSEM on a 2D P4estMesh. The code implements the compressible Euler equations with gamma=1.4, gravity g=1.0 in +y, domain [0,0.25] x [0,1], and time span [0,3]. Initial conditions match the Rayleigh-Taylor specification: density tanh profile across y=0.5, piecewise hydrostatic pressure, zero u, and the required single-mode v perturbation with sound speed and sin(pi*y)^6 factor. Boundary conditions are periodic in x and free-slip reflective walls at y=0 and y=1. Numerics use polydeg=4, surface flux flux_hllc, volume flux flux_ranocha inside selective Hennemann-Gassner shock capturing with alpha_max=0.2 and Lax-Friedrichs FV fallback, density-based Löhner AMR with ControllerThreeLevel base=4, med=5, max=6, and CFL=0.4 with RDPK3SpFSAL49. The code computes and prints mass drift, positivity extrema, symmetry error about x=0.125, wall-velocity compliance, energy-balance closure, NPZ sanity checks, optional polydeg=3 comparison, and a final line beginning exactly with 'Minimum achieved error:'. Visualization plots only rho and v at t=0.5,1.0,2.0,3.0 in a 2x4 layout with colorbar disabled and saves summary_all.png.
Proposed name:CompressibleEuler_RayleighTaylor_Periodic_Reflective\Question :Let's solve the 2D Compressible Euler equations for the Rayleigh-Taylor Instability.

**Physics Parameters:**
* **Equations:** Compressible Euler ($\gamma = 1.4$).
* **Gravity:** $g = 1.0$ acting in the **positive $y$-direction** (The source term adds momentum in $+y$).
* **Inviscid:** $\mu = 0$, thermal conductivity $k = 0$.

**Domain:**
$(x,y) \in [0, 0.25] \times [0, 1.0]$
Time interval: $t \in [0, 3.0]$

**Boundary Conditions:**
* $x$-direction: **Periodic**.
* $y$-direction: **Reflective / Free-slip walls** at $y=0$ and $y=1$ (No-penetration condition $v=0$).

**Initial Conditions:**
The domain is split at the interface $y=0.5$.
* **Density ($\rho$):** A smoothed interface between $\rho_{heavy} = 2.0$ (bottom) and $\rho_{light} = 1.0$ (top).
    $$ \rho(y) = \rho_{heavy} + (\rho_{light} - \rho_{heavy}) \cdot \frac{1}{2}\left(1 + \tanh\left(50(y - 0.5)\right)\right) $$
* **Pressure ($p$):** Hydrostatic balance ($\frac{dp}{dy} = \rho g$).
    $$ p(y) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1 & \text{if } y < 0.5 \\ y + 1.5 & \text{if } y \geq 0.5 \end{cases} $$
* **Velocity:** Initialized with zero horizontal velocity ($u=0$) and a perturbation in vertical velocity ($v$):
    $$ v(x,y) = -0.025 \cdot c \cdot \cos(8\pi x) \cdot \sin(\pi y)^6 $$
    Where $c = \sqrt{\gamma p / \rho}$ is the local speed of sound.

Create an appropiate Figure and saved it as summary_all.png

New Code (lines 82-182):
```julia
function npz_plot_axis(values)
    if ndims(values) == 1
        return Float64.(values)
    elseif ndims(values) == 2 &&
           (size(values, 1) == 1 || size(values, 2) == 1)
        return Float64.(vec(values))
    end
    return nothing
end

function npz_plot_node_arrays(pd)
    fields = ntuple(i -> Float64.(collect(pd.data[i])), 4)
    field_shape = size(fields[1])
    all(field -> size(field) == field_shape, fields) ||
        error("NPZ serialization: inconsistent primitive field layouts; " *
              "size.(pd.data)=$(size.(pd.data))")

    x_raw = collect(pd.x)
    y_raw = collect(pd.y)
    n_values = length(fields[1])

    # Node-wise coordinates and fields have identical storage ordering.
    if size(x_raw) == field_shape && size(y_raw) == field_shape
        values = ntuple(i -> vec(fields[i]), 4)
        return Float64.(vec(x_raw)), Float64.(vec(y_raw)), values
    end

    # Structured PlotData2D objects can store only the coordinate axes.
    # Expand both axes before flattening so every field value receives the
    # coordinate pair at the same matrix index.
    x_axis = npz_plot_axis(x_raw)
    y_axis = npz_plot_axis(y_raw)
    if x_axis !== nothing && y_axis !== nothing && length(field_shape) == 2
        nx = length(x_axis)
        ny = length(y_axis)

        if field_shape == (ny, nx)
            # Heatmap convention: rows correspond to y, columns to x.
            x_full = repeat(reshape(x_axis, 1, nx), ny, 1)
            y_full = repeat(reshape(y_axis, ny, 1), 1, nx)
        elseif field_shape == (nx, ny)
            # Dimension-major convention: rows correspond to x, columns to y.
            x_full = repeat(reshape(x_axis, nx, 1), 1, ny)
            y_full = repeat(reshape(y_axis, 1, ny), nx, 1)
        else
            error("NPZ serialization: coordinate axes do not match field layout; " *
                  "size(pd.x)=$(size(x_raw)), size(pd.y)=$(size(y_raw)), " *
                  "size.(pd.data)=$(size.(pd.data))")
        end

        values = ntuple(i -> vec(fields[i]), 4)
        return vec(x_full), vec(y_full), values
    end

    # Adaptive plot objects may store flat node-wise coordinate vectors while
    # retaining matrix containers for the primitive fields.
    if length(x_raw) == n_values && length(y_raw) == n_values
        values = ntuple(i -> vec(fields[i]), 4)
        return Float64.(vec(x_raw)), Float64.(vec(y_raw)), values
    end

    error("NPZ serialization: unsupported PlotData2D layout; " *
          "size(pd.x)=$(size(x_raw)), size(pd.y)=$(size(y_raw)), " *
          "size.(pd.data)=$(size.(pd.data))")
end

function print_physical_diagnostics!(integrator, x, y, rho, v, p)
    semi = integrator.p
    t = integrator.t
    # Use the DG quadrature for integral quantities rather than treating
    # nonuniformly spaced visualization nodes as a uniform grid.
    total_mass = Trixi.integrate(diagnostic_mass, integrator.u, semi)
    total_energy = Trixi.integrate(diagnostic_total_energy, integrator.u, semi)
    gravity_work = Trixi.integrate(diagnostic_gravity_work, integrator.u, semi)
    if !isfinite(initial_total_mass[])
        initial_total_mass[] = total_mass
    end
    mass_drift = total_mass - initial_total_mass[]
    relative_mass_drift = abs(mass_drift) /
                          max(abs(initial_total_mass[]), eps(Float64))
    rho_values = vec(rho)
    v_values = vec(v)
    p_values = vec(p)
    rho_min, rho_max = extrema(rho_values)
    p_min, p_max = extrema(p_values)
    symmetry_error = mirror_symmetry_error(x, y, rho_values)
    wall_velocity = 0.0
    max_abs_v = 0.0
    wall_samples = 0
    wall_tolerance = 1.0e-10
    for k in eachindex(y)
        max_abs_v = max(max_abs_v, abs(v_values[k]))
        if abs(y[k]) <= wall_tolerance ||
           abs(y[k] - 1.0) <= wall_tolerance
            wall_velocity = max(wall_velocity, abs(v_values[k]))
            wall_samples += 1
        end
    end
    if wall_samples == 0
        wall_velocity = NaN
        relative_wall_violation = NaN
    else
        relative_wall_violation = wall_velocity /
                                  max(max_abs_v, eps(Float64))
    end
    if isfinite(previous_energy_time[]) && t > previous_energy_time[]
        dE_dt = (total_energy - previous_total_energy[]) /
                (t - previous_energy_time[])
        energy_closure = abs(dE_dt - gravity_work) /
                         max(abs(dE_dt), abs(gravity_work), eps(Float64))
    else
        dE_dt = NaN
        energy_closure = NaN
    end
    record_verification_error!(:mass, relative_mass_drift)
    record_verification_error!(:symmetry, symmetry_error)
    record_verification_error!(:wall, relative_wall_violation)
    record_verification_error!(:energy_balance, energy_closure)
    println(Printf.@sprintf(
        "Diagnostics t=%.5f: dM=%.6e, rel_dM=%.6e, rho=[%.6e,%.6e], p=[%.6e,%.6e], sym=%.6e, wall_max_abs_v=%.6e, dE_dt=%.6e, gravity_work=%.6e, energy_closure=%.6e",
        t, mass_drift, relative_mass_drift,
        rho_min, rho_max, p_min, p_max, symmetry_error,
        wall_velocity, dE_dt, gravity_work, energy_closure))
    previous_energy_time[] = t
    previous_total_energy[] = total_energy
    return nothing
end

function save_npz_file(integrator)
    semi = integrator.p
    t = integrator.t
    # PeriodicCallback and its finalizer can both reach t_end.
    if isfinite(last_npz_save_time[]) &&
       abs(t - last_npz_save_time[]) <= 1.0e-10
        return nothing
    end
    pd = PlotData2D(integrator.u, semi, solution_variables=cons2prim)

    # Normalize coordinates and fields according to their actual PlotData2D
    # layout. No arrays are truncated and no validation checks are bypassed.
    x, y, primitive_fields = npz_plot_node_arrays(pd)
    rho, u, v, p = primitive_fields

    n_values = length(rho)
    if length(x) != n_values || length(y) != n_values ||
       length(u) != n_values || length(v) != n_values ||
       length(p) != n_values
        error("NPZ serialization: incompatible coordinate and field lengths")
    end

    filename = joinpath(npz_out, Printf.@sprintf("time_%08.5f.npz", t))
    npzwrite(filename, Dict{String, Any}(
        "t" => Float64(t),
        "x" => x, "y" => y,
        "rho" => rho, "u" => u, "v" => v, "p" => p))
    # Re-read the actual archive so the check covers serialization as well
    # as the in-memory PlotData2D reconstruction.
    verify_npz_file(filename)
    println("Saved and NPZ-verified: $(basename(filename))")
    print_physical_diagnostics!(integrator, x, y, rho, v, p)
    last_npz_save_time[] = t
    return nothing
end
```

**Physics Parameters:**

* **Gravity:** $g = 1.0$ acting in the **positive $y$-direction** (The source term adds momentum in $+y$).

The domain is split at the interface $y=0.5$.
* **Density ($\rho$):** A smoothed interface between $\rho_{heavy} = 2.0$ (bottom) and $\rho_{light} = 1.0$ (top).
    $$ \rho(y) = \rho_{heavy} + (\rho_{light} - \rho_{heavy}) \cdot \frac{1}{2}\left(1 + \tanh\left(50(y - 0.5)\right)\right) $$
* **Pressure ($p$):** Hydrostatic balance ($\frac{dp}{dy} = \rho g$).
    $$ p(y) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1 & \text{if } y < 0.5 \\ y + 1.5 & \text{if } y \geq 0.5 \end{cases} $$
* **Velocity:** Initialized with zero horizontal velocity ($u=0$) and a perturbation in vertical velocity ($v$):
    $$ v(x,y) = -0.025 \cdot c \cdot \cos(8\pi x) \cdot \sin(\pi y)^6 $$
    Where $c = \sqrt{\gamma p / \rho}$ is the local speed of sound.

```julia
function npz_plot_node_arrays(pd)
    x_raw = collect(pd.x)
    y_raw = collect(pd.y)
    data = pd.data

    # PlotData2DTriangulated stores one primitive state vector per plot node.
    # Extract variable i from every state; data[i] would instead select node i.
    # The length check also supports equivalent flattened nodal storage.
    if !(eltype(data) <: Number) &&
       length(data) == length(x_raw) &&
       length(data) == length(y_raw)
        fields = ntuple(i -> Float64.(getindex.(data, i)), 4)
        n_values = length(x_raw)
        all(field -> length(field) == n_values, fields) ||
            error("NPZ serialization: invalid triangulated primitive fields")

        # vec uses the same column-major node ordering for coordinates and fields.
        values = ntuple(i -> vec(fields[i]), 4)
        return Float64.(vec(x_raw)), Float64.(vec(y_raw)), values
    end

    # Structured PlotData2D stores the primitive variables as separate fields.
    fields = ntuple(i -> Float64.(collect(data[i])), 4)
    field_shape = size(fields[1])
    all(field -> size(field) == field_shape, fields) ||
        error("NPZ serialization: inconsistent primitive field layouts; " *
              "size.(pd.data)=$(size.(data))")

    n_values = length(fields[1])

    # Node-wise coordinates and fields have identical storage ordering.
    if size(x_raw) == field_shape && size(y_raw) == field_shape
        values = ntuple(i -> vec(fields[i]), 4)
        return Float64.(vec(x_raw)), Float64.(vec(y_raw)), values
    end

    # Structured PlotData2D objects can store only the coordinate axes.
    # Expand both axes before flattening so every field value receives the
    # coordinate pair at the same matrix index.
    x_axis = npz_plot_axis(x_raw)
    y_axis = npz_plot_axis(y_raw)
    if x_axis !== nothing && y_axis !== nothing && length(field_shape) == 2
        nx = length(x_axis)
        ny = length(y_axis)

        if field_shape == (ny, nx)
            # Heatmap convention: rows correspond to y, columns to x.
            x_full = repeat(reshape(x_axis, 1, nx), ny, 1)
            y_full = repeat(reshape(y_axis, ny, 1), 1, nx)
        elseif field_shape == (nx, ny)
            # Dimension-major convention: rows correspond to x, columns to y.
            x_full = repeat(reshape(x_axis, nx, 1), 1, ny)
            y_full = repeat(reshape(y_axis, 1, ny), nx, 1)
        else
            error("NPZ serialization: coordinate axes do not match field layout; " *
                  "size(pd.x)=$(size(x_raw)), size(pd.y)=$(size(y_raw)), " *
                  "size.(pd.data)=$(size.(data))")
        end

        values = ntuple(i -> vec(fields[i]), 4)
        return vec(x_full), vec(y_full), values
    end

    # Adaptive plot objects may store flat node-wise coordinate vectors while
    # retaining matrix containers for the primitive fields.
    if length(x_raw) == n_values && length(y_raw) == n_values
        values = ntuple(i -> vec(fields[i]), 4)
        return Float64.(vec(x_raw)), Float64.(vec(y_raw)), values
    end

    error("NPZ serialization: unsupported PlotData2D layout; " *
          "size(pd.x)=$(size(x_raw)), size(pd.y)=$(size(y_raw)), " *
          "size.(pd.data)=$(size.(data))")
end
```

## Section 1: Status Decision

**Status:** `REJECTED_FOR_REFINEMENT`

The run is stable, physically credible, and substantially improved over the previous 81-point attempt. However, it is not perfect: the final symmetry and energy-balance diagnostics remain non-negligible, and no discretization-convergence comparison was available.

The saturation rule does **not** apply. The only previous scored trial achieved **81**, so there is no earlier run with score ≥90 from which to count five subsequent refinement attempts.

## Section 2: Quantitative Scorecard

- **Score Integrity:** 50/50
- **Score Accuracy:** 18/20
- **Score Details:** 12/15
- **Score Optimality:** 15/15
- **TOTAL SCORE:** **95/100**

## Section 3: Forensic Analysis & Feedback

### Observation

The summary plot shows the expected Rayleigh–Taylor evolution:

- At \(t=0.5\) and \(t=1.0\), the initially horizontal material interface develops a centered, growing heavy-fluid protrusion.
- At \(t=2.0\), the heavy spike has risen to approximately \(y\approx0.7\)–\(0.75\), with a mushroom-shaped head and flank roll-up consistent with Kelvin–Helmholtz instability.
- At \(t=3.0\), the spike approaches the upper wall and develops narrower, multiscale plume structures. The vertical-velocity field shows the corresponding rising central column and surrounding return flow.
- There are no obvious blank panels, NaN regions, horizontal white-stripe rendering artifacts, or large-amplitude Gibbs oscillations. The HLLC/polydeg-4 upgrade and visualization repair were therefore successful.

The final diagnostics confirm excellent discrete mass conservation and positivity:

- Relative mass drift: \(2.55\times10^{-12}\)
- \(\rho\in[0.752219,2.662292]\)
- \(p\in[1.223693,2.651350]\)
- Final wall violation: \(\max|v|=6.12\times10^{-4}\)

Two final-time verification issues remain:

1. **Symmetry:** `sym=1.389356e-02`, approximately **1.39%**, despite the symmetric initial condition and expected mirror symmetry.
2. **Energy balance:** `energy_closure=8.956329e-02`, approximately **8.96%**, with `dE_dt=4.804714e-02` versus `gravity_work=5.277373e-02`.

The requested exact log line is:

```text
Minimum achieved error: 0.000000e+00 (mass); verification minima=Dict(:symmetry => 5.064360808406431e-16, :wall => 1.4507324084663821e-90, :mass => 0.0, :energy_balance => 0.0006950559846064934)
```

**Quantitative error is not applicable as no ground truth is available.** The printed zero is the minimum mass-conservation diagnostic, **not** an error against an exact Rayleigh–Taylor solution. Likewise, the reported symmetry, wall, and energy values are minima over the verification history and do not represent the more restrictive final-time values.

No archived polydeg-3 states were accessible, so the run provides no actual p3–p4 convergence measurement.

### Diagnosis

The primary remaining concerns are **late-time resolution/symmetry drift and incomplete verification closure**, not numerical instability.

The late-time density structures appear increasingly fine and somewhat irregular, so visual inspection alone cannot certify that the roll-up and contact interfaces are grid-converged. The symmetry drift may arise from asymmetric AMR refinement/coarsening, mesh partitioning, or genuine amplification of small numerical perturbations in the nonlinear instability.

The energy discrepancy should not immediately be interpreted as a solver source-term error. Comparing a finite-difference \(dE/dt\), evaluated between relatively widely separated saves, with an **instantaneous** gravity-work integral can produce precisely such a closure mismatch during rapid late-time evolution. The diagnostic must be made temporally consistent before using it to justify additional dissipation or mesh refinement.

No exact-method architecture override is warranted: the full nonlinear, two-dimensional, wall-bounded RTI evolution with gravity and vortex roll-up has no feasible closed-form analytical solution. DGSEM with HLLC, Ranocha volume flux, density-based AMR, and selective stabilization remains an appropriate high-fidelity approach.

### Targeted Improvements

- **For Integrity:** None. The run completed successfully, generated readable plots, and maintained finite, positive states.

- **For Accuracy:**
  - Replace the present energy check with a **cumulative balance**,
    \[
    E(t)-E(0)-\int_0^t\!\int_\Omega \rho g v\,dV\,d\tau,
    \]
    accumulating gravity work at every time step or at least at intervals much smaller than \(0.1\). Use consistent quadrature and AMR cell weights for both terms.
  - Verify symmetry on a common, mirror-paired visualization/quadrature grid. Use a mirror-symmetric base mesh and symmetric AMR treatment where possible; do not artificially average the solution to hide asymmetry.
  - Archive the current polydeg-4 NPZ states so the next run can perform the missing controlled convergence comparison.

- **For Details:**
  - Perform one controlled **polydeg \(4\rightarrow5\)** refinement on the same base mesh and AMR levels, retaining HLLC/Ranocha and selective shock capturing. Reduce **CFL to 0.3** for the higher-degree run.
  - Compare polydeg-5 against the archived polydeg-4 results at common times on an identical uniform grid, using density differences and spike-tip position. Particular attention should be paid to \(t=2\) and \(t=3\), where the present solution is most likely under-resolved.
  - Do not globally increase LF dissipation or revert to HLL; neither is justified by the current plots. If polydeg-5 introduces local ringing, adjust only the local blending activation/positivity safeguard.

- **For Optimality:** None. Retain the present DGSEM/HLLC/entropy-stable-volume-flux architecture.

--- Strategy Report Received ---
**1. Problem Statement**

Single-mode compressible Rayleigh–Taylor instability: heavy fluid (ρ=2) beneath light fluid (ρ=1) in a gravitational field acting in +y, seeded by a single-mode vertical velocity perturbation. The interface grows through linear amplification into a nonlinear rising heavy spike with Kelvin–Helmholtz roll-up, to be resolved on $t\in[0,3]$.

**1. Mathematical Specification (Code-Ready Extraction)**

* **Governing Equations:** 2D Compressible Euler with gravity source:
$$\partial_t \begin{pmatrix} \rho \\ \rho u \\ \rho v \\ E \end{pmatrix} + \partial_x \begin{pmatrix} \rho u \\ \rho u^2 + p \\ \rho u v \\ u(E+p) \end{pmatrix} + \partial_y \begin{pmatrix} \rho v \\ \rho u v \\ \rho v^2 + p \\ v(E+p) \end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix} 0 \\ 0 \\ \rho g \\ \rho g v \end{pmatrix}$$
with $p = (\gamma-1)\left(E - \tfrac{1}{2}\rho(u^2+v^2)\right)$, $\gamma = 1.4$, $g = 1.0$ (momentum source in $+y$; energy source $\rho g v$).

* **Domain:** $(x,y) \in [0, 0.25] \times [0, 1.0]$, $t \in [0, 3.0]$.

* **Boundary Conditions:** $x$: Periodic. $y=0$ and $y=1$: Reflective/free-slip walls (no-penetration, $v=0$).

* **Initial Conditions:**
$$\rho(x,y,0) = 2.0 + (1.0 - 2.0)\cdot\frac{1}{2}\left[1 + \tanh\!\left(50(y-0.5)\right)\right]$$
$$p(x,y,0) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1, & y < 0.5 \\ y + 1.5, & y \geq 0.5 \end{cases}$$
$$u(x,y,0) = 0$$
$$v(x,y,0) = -0.025\, c(x,y)\, \cos(8\pi x)\, \left[\sin(\pi y)\right]^6, \qquad c(x,y) = \sqrt{\frac{\gamma\, p(x,y,0)}{\rho(x,y,0)}}$$
$$E(x,y,0) = \frac{p}{\gamma - 1} + \frac{1}{2}\rho\left(u^2 + v^2\right)$$

* **Constants & Parameters:** $\gamma = 1.4$; $\mathbf{g} = [0, 1.0]^T$; $\rho_{heavy}=2.0$, $\rho_{light}=1.0$; interface at $y=0.5$ (tanh smoothing 50); perturbation amplitude $0.025$; mode $8\pi$ (exactly one period across $x$-width); $t_{end}=3.0$.

* **Expected Physics:** Exponential linear growth of the single mode ($\lambda_x = 0.25$); nonlinear saturation near $t\approx 1$; heavy spike rising with mushroom cap and KH roll-up on the flanks by $t\approx 2$; multiscale secondary plumes with the spike approaching the top wall by $t=3$. Dominant features: contact discontinuity (density interface) and shear layers; weak compressibility.

**2. Strategic Methodology**

* **Exact solution feasible?** **No.** MoC applies only to 1D simple-wave hyperbolic systems; here we have a fully coupled 2D nonlinear system, a gravity source, reflecting walls, and an instability that amplifies the seed through a nonlinear cascade into vortex roll-up — characteristics cannot describe the post-saturation interface. Linearized RTI theory yields only the early-time growth rate, not the $t\in[0,3]$ evolution. No separation of variables or Green's function exists for the nonlinear convective flux.
* **Selection:** Fourier Spectral is precluded (walls in $y$; developing contact/shear layers → Gibbs). Steady CG is inapplicable (transient). **Select: DGSEM in 2D plus time** — high-order, entropy-stable, mixed-BC capable, AMR-ready for the multiscale roll-up. This is the appropriate high-fidelity architecture; the Advisor confirms no exact-method override is warranted.

**3. Implementation Requirements (CRITICAL)**

* **Configuration:** Use template defaults for all unspecified parameters. The Advisor-mandated overrides below **supersede** all defaults.

* **3.0.1 Adaptive Mesh Refinement & Shock Capturing — Variable Selection Analysis:**
    * *Signal-to-noise:* **Density ($\rho$)** is the material-interface tracer — it remains sharp and well-defined exactly at the features of interest (interface, spike, bubble, mushroom flanks). High SNR, feature-localized. **SELECT density for AMR (Löhner indicator).**
    * *Rejected:* Velocity becomes globally chaotic in the KH roll-up (refining on it wastes resolution domain-wide). Pressure is dominated by the smooth hydrostatic background (poor feature contrast, spurious triggers). Derived quantities (Mach, entropy) are noisier than $\rho$.
    * *Shock capturing:* retain **selective local blending** (LF flux, $\alpha_{max}=0.2$). Do NOT increase global dissipation.

* **3.1 Advisor-Mandated Adjustments (Overrides):**
    1. **polydeg = 5** (upgrade from 4), on the **same base mesh and AMR levels** (initial refinement 3; controller base=4, med=5, max=6, interval 20).
    2. **CFL = 0.3** (reduced from 0.4) for the higher-degree run.
    3. **Retain HLLC surface flux + Ranocha entropy-stable volume flux.** Do NOT revert to HLL; do NOT globally increase LF dissipation.
    4. If polydeg-5 introduces local ringing, adjust **only** the local blending activation threshold / positivity safeguard.
    5. **Cumulative energy balance:** replace the finite-difference $dE/dt$ check with $E(t) - E(0) - \int_0^t\!\!\int_\Omega \rho g v \, dV \, d\tau$, accumulating gravity work at **every time step** with consistent quadrature and AMR cell weights.
    6. **Symmetry verification** on a common, mirror-paired quadrature grid; mirror-symmetric base mesh and symmetric AMR treatment; do NOT average the solution to hide asymmetry.
    7. **Archive polydeg-5 NPZ states**; load archived polydeg-4 states (previous run) if accessible for the controlled p4→p5 comparison (see §5).
    8. Print final verification summary beginning with `Minimum achieved error:`.

**4. Visualization Strategy**

* **Field Selection:** $\rho$ (primary driver — buoyancy from the density contrast; tracks interface/spike/bubble) and $v$ (vertical velocity — rising heavy column and return flow). These two capture the dominant dynamics; omit $u, p, E$ to keep panels readable.
* **Task:** Plot $\rho$ and $v$ at $t = 0.5,\ 1.0,\ 2.0,\ 3.0$.
* **Layout:** Grid — 2 rows (fields) × 4 columns (times).
* **Saving Data:** Save the **full solution state** (all variables) at `dt_save = 0.1` for Ground Truth; plotting selection affects only the summary image.
* **Constraints:** Save as `summary_all.png` (strictly). **DISABLE COLORBARS** (`colorbar=false`) — Julia. Automatic color scales; no fixed colormaps. Plot only primary variables; no derived quantities.

**5. Cross-Validation & Data Integration (Conditional)**

* No external data paths in [USER_QUESTION] → no mandatory external reference.
* **Conditional (Advisor-mandated):** If archived polydeg-4 NPZ states from the previous run are accessible, load them, interpolate both solutions onto an **identical uniform grid** at common save times, and print the **relative $L_2$ density error** and **spike-tip position difference**, with particular attention to $t=2.0$ and $t=3.0$.

**REQUIRED COMPONENTS**
